=== PROMPT ===
Here is some python code and the inputs passed into it.
Determine the output by simulating the execution.

Code: python
def _input(x):
        return name_lookup(x) or func_lookup(x) or default
Inputs: 0, 0, 86400

Reason in a direct trace-first way.

A productive pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Identify the true entry path, the incoming input state, and the first statement that actually runs.
- Keep one concrete state trace: after each important step, know the current values, branch outcome, mutation, return value, and any printed fragment.
- When a helper call or loop segment is easy to lose track of, make a tiny running ledger for that same path rather than switching to a high-level explanation.
- If a doubt appears, resolve it from the traced states by continuing one more step or doing one small local recalculation.
- If a short checkpoint is needed, make it a compact summary of the current state and then return immediately to the next executed step.
- Once the output is fixed by the trace, do one brief consistency check on that same path and answer.

Answer using <ans></ans> tags only.

```
class VirtualMachine(object):
    def __init__(self):
        self.frames = []
        self.return_value = None
        self.last_exception = None
    ...
    def name_lookup(self, name):
        frame = self.frames[-1]
        while frame:
            if name in frame.locals:
                return frame.locals[name]
            if name in frame.globals:
                return frame.globals[name]
            frame = frame.f_back
        return self.builtins.get(name, None)
```

Something like that. And `func_lookup` might be similar but for functions? Hmm.

In Python's `dis` module, when disassembling bytecode, each instruction has an opcode and an argument. The argument can be interpreted differently depending on the opcode:
- If opcode in `hasconst`, argument is an index into `co_consts` -> use `_get_const`.
- If opcode in `hasname`, argument is an index into `co_names` -> use `_get_name`.
- If opcode in `hasjrel` or `hasjabs`, argument is a jump target.
- If opcode in `haslocal`, argument is an index into `co_varnames`.
- If opcode in `hascompare`, argument is an index into `cmp_op`.
- If opcode in `hasfree`, argument is an index into `co_freevars`/`co_cellvars`.
- Else, argument is just an integer.

There is no `func_lookup`.
